**Table 5.** Summary of multiple deep learning methods for vehicle detection.

| Ref. | Method | Outcomes | Advantages | Limitations |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| [[34](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B34-applsci-12-06831)] | 3D vehicle bounding box mapping | 65.52% more enhancement in 50 m than LiDAR. | Exceeded the outcomes of LiDAR in long range. | Unstable accuracy for certain error properties. |
| [[35](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B35-applsci-12-06831),[36](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B36-applsci-12-06831)] | YOLO V2 and K-mean ++ | mAP 94.78% in 0.038 s. | Faster detection and recognition. | Trained with vehicle types of data. |
| [[37](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B37-applsci-12-06831)] | AdaBoost with pixel lookup features | 81.94% accuracy in best-case scenarios. | Improved result with occlusion pattern by clustering. | Performance decreased when 70% overlap and heavy occlusion. |
| [[38](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B38-applsci-12-06831)] | Deep CNN and AdaBoost | 99.50% accuracy in 0.028 s. | Highest accuracy in daytime and trained with low-quality frames. | Not applicable in low-light or complex scenarios. |
| [[39](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B39-applsci-12-06831)] | CNN with Caffe framework | 96.47% accuracy in 51.28 ms. | Fast classification in low resolution input. | Higher execution time compared to other methods. |
| [[40](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B40-applsci-12-06831)] | ANN and Kalman Filter | Recall 78.48% and precision 90.78% in urban scenario. | Nighttime taillight-based detection in urban area. | Decreased in performance for headlight illumination in short distance. |
| [[41](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B41-applsci-12-06831)] | SI-NET | 90.60% accuracy in best-case scenarios. | Improved the limitation-scale insensitivity of CNN. | Not applicable in poor lighting, nighttime or complex weather scenarios. |
| [[42](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B42-applsci-12-06831)] | Faster-RCNN with AGO | 87.2% average precision in 0.055 s. | Enable to detect vehicle in large, medium and short field. | Not applicable in urban or challenging lighting conditions. |
| [[43](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B43-applsci-12-06831)] | Faster R-CNN with RPN | Highest 84.4% accuracy for car detection in 81 ms. | Outperformed Faster R-CNN in terms of execution time. | Unsatisfactory accuracy compared with the obtained execution time. |
| [[44](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B44-applsci-12-06831)] | Lightweight YOLO Network | 86.81% and 78.91% precision and recall within 44.5 ms, respectively. | Applicable in both day and night scenarios in multiple FoV. | Was not tested in urban or crowded environment. |
| [[45](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B45-applsci-12-06831)] | Quicker region-based CNN | 84.97% mAP nighttime scenarios. | Able to detect in low-light and almost no-light conditions. | Required huge manual data labelling. |

**Table 6.** Summary of multiple deep learning methods for traffic sign and light recognition.

| Ref. | Method | Outcomes | Advantages | Limitations |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| [[46](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B46-applsci-12-06831)] | BCNN | 98.52% accuracy for low visibility of road signs. | Branch-output mechanism enhanced recognition speed and accuracy. | Implementation from moving platform was not tested. |
| [[47](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B47-applsci-12-06831)] | LetNet-5 based CNN | 99.95% accuracy. | Lightweight color segmentation before classification, improved processing speed. | Detected two traffic signs for having different location of proposed region. |
| [[48](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B48-applsci-12-06831)] | CNN with Gabor filter | Accuracy of 99.75% in 5.4 ms. | Obtained highest accuracy and enhanced the performance of LetNet-5. | Performance decreased in complicated backgrounds. |
| [[49](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B49-applsci-12-06831)] | Weighted multi CNN | Highest 99.75% accuracy in 10 ms. | Classification in high-speed driving and outperformed in low-light conditions. | Struggled to classify in challenging blurry vision condition. |
| [[50](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B50-applsci-12-06831)] | SVM | Recall score 97% and 99% for two cases. | Faster detection of prohibitory and danger signs in poor illumination. | Only applied to red circular signs. |
| [[51](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B51-applsci-12-06831)] | YOLO V2 | Highest 96.81% AUC values in 0.017 s. | Faster and simple processing pipeline. | Decrease in performance for small traffic signs. |
| [[52](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B52-applsci-12-06831)] | CapsNet | 98.72% accuracy. | Resolved loss of max pooling to boost CNN’s performance. | Did not consider complex lighting condition |
| [[53](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B53-applsci-12-06831)] | Fast R-CNN | 100% recall and 92% average precision. | Detected traffic light as well as indicating signs. | Showed high false positive. |
| [[54](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B54-applsci-12-06831)] | DeepTLR | Highest 93.5% F1 score. | Did not require position details or temporal principles. | Lower precision rate. |
| [[55](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B55-applsci-12-06831)] | OCF | 11.5% improved AUC value for green light. | Better detection in complex scenes with low luminous objects combining. | Unsatisfied accuracy for red left traffic light identification. |
| [[56](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B56-applsci-12-06831)] | YOLO | 42.03% and 49.16% mAP higher on two datasets. | Took upper half of frame to eliminate search area and vehicle taillights. | Did not deploy in nighttime traffic scene where reflection creates confusion. |
| [[57](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B57-applsci-12-06831)] | YOLO | 99% in 0.06 s. | 3D localization and tracking of traffic lights in large field of view. | Required more labelling. |

**Table 7.** Summary of multiple deep learning methods for pedestrian detection.

| Ref. | Method | Outcomes | Advantages | Limitations |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| [[60](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B60-applsci-12-06831)] | DNN | 35.85 on the average miss rate. | Processed in large field images, continuous detection in complex scenes. | Higher missing rate with comparatively slow detection |
| [[61](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B61-applsci-12-06831)] | YOLOv3-TINY and DeepSort | 80.3% accuracy in complex environment. | Designed faster warning area bounding by each pedestrian with direction labelling. | Only considered daytime scenarios and lower accuracy. |
| [[63](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B63-applsci-12-06831)] | CNN | 26% and 14% missing rates on two datasets, respectively. | Did not require explicit detection in crowded scenario. | Did not apply for motion images or real-time problems. |
| [[64](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B64-applsci-12-06831)] | Part-level FCN and CNN | 6.83% improved outcomes compared with CifarNet. | Estimated accurate distances of pedestrians generating the confidence map using FCN. | High missing rate for practical implementation. |
| [[65](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B65-applsci-12-06831)] | SSD-based late-fusion CNN | Decreased by more than 10% of log average miss rate. | Most applicable in nighttime implementation. | Slower detection and complex parameter tuning. |
| [[66](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B66-applsci-12-06831)] | Resolution aware-based CNN | 3.3% lower log-average miss rate than CNN. | Learnt feature extraction from low-level image with resolution information. | Was not applied in complex traffic or crowded environment. |
| [[67](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B67-applsci-12-06831)] | Faster R-CNN | 86.6% average precision in 0.104 s. | Outperformed on distorted and blurry frames. | Did not consider low-light or traffic scenarios. |
| [[68](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B68-applsci-12-06831)] | R-CNN with ACF model | 14.1%, 15.3%, 45.6% miss-rate on three datasets. | Reduced the number of region proposals, and costs less time. | ---- |
| [[72](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B72-applsci-12-06831)] | Dark channel-based SVM | 81.63% precision and 67.88% recall. | Pedestrian detection and position estimation from haze condition. | Presumed constant depths in input images. |
| [[73](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B73-applsci-12-06831)] | Laplacian distribution model | 4.1 mAP and 3.98 mAP on expert and ordinary view, respectively. | Pedestrian detection in complex dim-light condition. | Was not applied in real-time driving scenes. |
| [[75](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B75-applsci-12-06831)] | Multiple YOLO methods | Highest average precision 80.5% in 81.7 ms. | Minimized number of parameters and outperformed state-of-art methods. | Early dependency on preliminary boxes during detection process. |
| [[76](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B76-applsci-12-06831)] | GPCANet | 96.2% recall in 320 ms on SCUT dataset. | Improved outcomes in both day and night including far FoV and crowded traffic. | Higher log average missing rate for occluded on-road objects. |
| [[77](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B77-applsci-12-06831)] | CNN | Showed 5.5% miss rate. | Localized distracting pedestrian and improved detection annotations. | Did not test in cases for crowded or complex scenes. |
| [[78](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B78-applsci-12-06831)] | CNN cascaded with AdaBoost | Generated the maximum 9% log-average miss rate. | Combined thermal images for nighttime detection. | Might fail in complex urban traffic scenarios. |

**Table 8.** Summary of multiple deep learning methods for lane detection and tracking.

| Ref. | Method | Outcomes | Advantages | Limitations |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| [[79](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B79-applsci-12-06831)] | Modified CNN | 95.5% accuracy for single frame. | Reduced dependency of manual labelling and processing time in single frame. | Deployment for testing did not consider urban or crowded scenarios. |
| [[80](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B80-applsci-12-06831)] | U-Net | 95.57% max F-measurement in 0.15 s. | Smooth segmentation of road surface with multiple objects as obstacles. | Huge dependency on manual pre-labelling. |
| [[81](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B81-applsci-12-06831)] | Multitask CNN and RNN | Max AUC value of 99%. | Recognized region in complex traffic and visualized spatially distributed cues. | Higher computational cost and inefficient for large field of view. |
| [[82](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B82-applsci-12-06831)] | Bayesian Model | 91.83% true positive rate. | Automated curve detection in rural and challenging roads with lower error rate. | Lighting conditions were not considered and slow processing. |
| [[83](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B83-applsci-12-06831)] | UNet and Kalman Filter | 2.5% and 9.75% lateral error generated in 10 ms. | Obtained less lateral error and overcame slow feature extraction. | Limited to a simple close-loop circle in TORCS simulator. |
| [[88](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B88-applsci-12-06831)] | CondLaneNet | 86.10% F1 score. | Solved the lane detection in fork and dense scenarios. | Contextual features need to be refined. |
| [[90](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B90-applsci-12-06831)] | RANSAC | 99.9% precision and 99.4% F-measurement. | Prevent misdetection by estimating parameters when illumination changes. | Slower execution, slightly high FPR and did not consider urban traffic road. |
| [[91](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B91-applsci-12-06831)] | CNN | Obtained highest accuracy (97.85%). | Outperformed in shadow and roads with obstacles. | Created errors during shifting the ground to predict disappearing point |
| [[92](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B92-applsci-12-06831)] | Modified CNN | Average 99.25% accuracy. | Most suitable deployment in two datasets with faster runtime for nine classes | Did not test in real-time driving senses. |
| [[93](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B93-applsci-12-06831)] | CNN-LSTM with SegNet | 96.78% accuracy in 46 ms. | Raised performance in occlusion scenarios. | Combined method of CNN and RNN were resource hungry and slow. |
| [[94](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B94-applsci-12-06831)] | Modified Decision forest and DNN | 98.80% and 97.45% accuracy, respectively. | High accuracy in surface and road detection. | High computational cost. |
| [[95](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B95-applsci-12-06831)] | CNN | 65.2% mIoU, 79.6% class average accuracy. | Modified CNN to achieve low complexity and maintained similar accuracy. | Was not tested in crowded traffic environment. |
| [[96](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B96-applsci-12-06831)] | EL-GAN | 96.39% accuracy. | The loss of embedding in detector boosted the performance closest to the mark. | Required tuning of huge number of parameters. |
| [[98](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B98-applsci-12-06831)] | LaneNet and HNet | 96.4% accuracy within 19 ms. | Did not require post processing, pixel-wise segmentation or fix lane number. | Faced challenges in long field of view while detecting curves. |
| [[99](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B99-applsci-12-06831)] | EL-CNN | 98.9% accuracy. | Reduced training time 1/50 and 1/200 in KNU and Caltech datasets, respectively. | Required matrix inversion for better execution time in high dimensional data. |
| [[100](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B100-applsci-12-06831)] | ERFNet-DLSF | 95.80% accuracy. | Did not need post processing to estimate line coordinates using weight map. | Was not tested in urban or complex lighting conditions. |
| [[101](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B101-applsci-12-06831)] | CNN with SAD | 96.64% accuracy. | Has self-learning ability and increased the efficiency of narrow-lane detection. | Complex hyperparameter adjustment for inadequate training process. |
| [[44](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B44-applsci-12-06831)] | Lightweight YOLO Network | 90.32% precision and 83.76% recall in 50.6 ms. | Applicable in both day and night scenarios and multiple fields of view. | High interruption for obscured vehicles. |

| [[102](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B102-applsci-12-06831)] | Markov Chain Monte Carlo | Identify intersections with 90% accuracy. | Identified intersections from challenging and crowded urban scenario. | Independent tractlets caused unpredictable collision in complex scenarios. |

| [[105](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B105-applsci-12-06831)] | DeepLabV3 and ResNet-50 | 79% mIoU with 50% less labelled dataset. | Reduce dependency on huge labelled data with softmax fine-tuning. | Dependency on labelled dataset. |
| [[106](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B106-applsci-12-06831)] | Multistage Deep CNN | Highest 92.90% accuracy. | Less model complexity and three times less time complexity than GoogleNet. | Did not demonstrate for challenging scenes. |

| [[108](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B108-applsci-12-06831)] | 2D-LSTM with RNN | 78.52% accuracy. | Able to avoid the confusion of ambiguous labels by increasing the contrast. | Suffered scene segmentation in foggy vision. |
| [[109](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B109-applsci-12-06831)] | CDN | Achieved 80.5% mean IoU. | Fixed image semantic information and outperformed expressive spatial feature. | Unable to focus on each object in low-resolution images. |

| [[111](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B111-applsci-12-06831)] | CNN with pyramid pooling | Scored 54.5 mIoU. | Developed novel image augmentation technique from fisheye images. | Not applicable for far field of view. |
| [[112](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B112-applsci-12-06831)] | Spatial CNN | 96.53% accuracy and 68.2% mIoU. | Re-architected CNN for long continuous road and traffic scenarios. | Performance dropped significantly during low-light and rainy scenarios. |
| [[113](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B113-applsci-12-06831)] | OP-DNN | 91.1% accuracy after 7000 iterations. | Decreased the issue of overfitting in small-scale training set. | Required re-weighting for improved result but inapplicable in uncertain environment. |
| [[114](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B114-applsci-12-06831)] | CNN and LSTM | 90% accuracy in 3 s. | Predict risk of accidents lane merging, tollgate and unsigned intersections. | Slower computational time and tested in similar kinds of traffic scenes. |
| [[116](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B116-applsci-12-06831)] | DNN | 68.95% accuracy and 77% recall. | Determined risk of class from traffic scene. | Sensitivity analysis was not used for crack detection. |
| [[117](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B117-applsci-12-06831)] | Graph-Q and DeepScene-Q | Obtained p-value of 0.0011. | Developed dynamic interaction-aware-based scene understanding for AVS. | Unable to see fast lane result and slow performance of agent. |
| [[118](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B118-applsci-12-06831)] | PCA with CNN | High accuracy for transverse classification. | Identified damages and cracks in the road, without pre-processing. | Required manual labelling which was time consuming. |
| [[119](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B119-applsci-12-06831)] | CNN | 92.51%, 89.65% recall and F1 score, respectively. | Automatic learning feature and tested in complex background. | Had not performed in real-time driving environment. |
| [[120](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B120-applsci-12-06831)] | SegNet and SqueezedNet | Highest accuracy (98.93%) in GAPs dataset. | Identified potholes with texture-reliant approach. | Failed cases due to confusing with texture of the restoration patches. |

| [[122](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B122-applsci-12-06831)] | Weighted hybrid SVM | Max 94.7% accuracy for lane changing task. | Faster decision making in different traffic conditions. | Yet to demonstrate for critical and uncertain driving scenes. |
| [[131](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B131-applsci-12-06831),[132](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B132-applsci-12-06831)] | AC-DRL with DDPG | 1.43% collision rate. | Autonomous braking system while lane shifting with high accuracy. | Complexity with large training dataset and decreased the convergence rate. |

| [[128](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B128-applsci-12-06831)] | MOMD + SARSOP | 91.67% les collision and 25.9% enhanced response rate. | Efficient overtaking decision without rule-based system and optimum actions. | Did not consider real-time speed and distance value. |
| [[129](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B129-applsci-12-06831)] | MGRL | Almost 100% safety index to reach goal without collision. | Outperformed overtaking, collision avoiding, arriving at seven RL goals. | Insignificant performance to keep lane while overtaking. |

| [[132](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B132-applsci-12-06831)] | LSTM with Fuzzy Logic | 92.40% accuracy. | Decision based on the behavior of surrounding vehicles. | Urban or real-life traffic conditions were not considered. |
| [[133](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B133-applsci-12-06831)] | GBDT | Calibration approach scored 0.3517 MAD. | Understand the key impacts of multiple variables on acceleration of fusion. | Implemented on old dataset and driver features were not analyzed. |
| [[134](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B134-applsci-12-06831)] | DRL using Q-masking | 91% success rate with 24% lower collision rate. | Effective on high-level policies learning through conventional optimization. | Did not analyze real-time and complex road challenges. |
| [[135](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B135-applsci-12-06831)] | Rule-based DQN policy | Safety rate 0.8 on average speed and lane-changing time. | Productive data alternative to end-to-end policy learning on challenge for high-level policy. | Required explicit driving path while training that caused low performance in complex scenes. |
| [[136](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B136-applsci-12-06831)] | DQN | Collision rate decreased from 61.29% to 0%. | Efficient and quick emergency braking in complex environment. | Ambiguous training and environment setting. |
| [[137](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B137-applsci-12-06831)] | HMGRM | Achieved specificity 97.41% and 89.41% accuracy. | Highly accurate braking action from driving situation. | Analysis of kinematic characteristics for both host and lead vehicle was missing. |
| [[138](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B138-applsci-12-06831)] | DRQN | Obtained maximum 64.48% more reward than human. | Did not require a labelled dataset and only took screen pixel as input. | Training time consuming. |
| [[139](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B139-applsci-12-06831)] | AGC based DRL | 98.69% higher average mean reward at intersection. | Efficient on intersection scenarios and reduced DRL training time. | Showed few collisions and unfinished cases during intersection traverse. |
| [[140](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B140-applsci-12-06831)] | CNN + DDPG | Lowest 0.48 SD for left turn across path. | Overcame drawback of single scenario with discrete behavior in intersections. | Only considered lateral maneuvers. |
| [[141](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B141-applsci-12-06831)] | DRQN | 70% success rate for collision free episodes. | Tackled high-level behavioral decision in pedestrian-filled environment. | Low accuracy. |
| [[142](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B142-applsci-12-06831)] | DDQL and FRC | Maximum 94% accuracy with stable reward. | Applied for four driving decision for navigation avoiding obstacle. | Limited decision making for vehicles in parallel sides. |
| [[143](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B143-applsci-12-06831)] | RL + MCTS | 100% collision free episodes. | Outperformed DRQN and MOBIL method for safe lane shifting | Did not consider urban scenarios. |

**Table 14.** Evaluation techniques.

| [[40](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B40-applsci-12-06831),[44](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B44-applsci-12-06831),[50](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B50-applsci-12-06831),[53](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B53-applsci-12-06831),[72](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B72-applsci-12-06831),[76](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B76-applsci-12-06831),[82](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B82-applsci-12-06831),[90](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B90-applsci-12-06831),[110](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B110-applsci-12-06831),[116](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B116-applsci-12-06831),[119](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B119-applsci-12-06831),[120](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B120-applsci-12-06831),[137](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B137-applsci-12-06831)] | Sensitivity/TPR/Recall (R) | $R = \frac{T P}{T P + F N}$ | TP is the true positive and FN is the false negative detection. |
| [[40](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B40-applsci-12-06831),[44](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B44-applsci-12-06831),[67](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B67-applsci-12-06831),[72](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B72-applsci-12-06831),[90](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B90-applsci-12-06831),[110](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B110-applsci-12-06831),[116](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B116-applsci-12-06831),[119](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B119-applsci-12-06831),[120](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B120-applsci-12-06831)] | Precision (P) | $P = \frac{T P}{T P + F P}$ | FP is the false positive detection. |
| [[54](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B54-applsci-12-06831),[80](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B80-applsci-12-06831),[88](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B88-applsci-12-06831),[90](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B90-applsci-12-06831),[110](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B110-applsci-12-06831),[119](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B119-applsci-12-06831),[120](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B120-applsci-12-06831),[176](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B176-applsci-12-06831)] | F1 Score | $F 1 = 2 * \left(\frac{R \times P}{R + P}\right)$ | - |

[[37](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B37-applsci-12-06831),[38](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B38-applsci-12-06831),[39](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B39-applsci-12-06831),[41](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B41-applsci-12-06831),[43](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B43-applsci-12-06831),[46](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B46-applsci-12-06831),[47](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B47-applsci-12-06831),[48](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B48-applsci-12-06831),[49](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B49-applsci-12-06831),[52](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B52-applsci-12-06831),[57](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B57-applsci-12-06831),[60](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B60-applsci-12-06831),[61](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B61-applsci-12-06831),[64](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B64-applsci-12-06831),[79](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B79-applsci-12-06831),[91](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B91-applsci-12-06831),[92](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B92-applsci-12-06831),[93](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B93-applsci-12-06831),[94](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B94-applsci-12-06831),[96](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B96-applsci-12-06831),[98](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B98-applsci-12-06831),[99](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B99-applsci-12-06831),[100](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B100-applsci-12-06831),[101](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B101-applsci-12-06831),[106](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B106-applsci-12-06831),[108](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B108-applsci-12-06831),[112](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B112-applsci-12-06831),[113](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B113-applsci-12-06831),[114](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B114-applsci-12-06831),[118](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B118-applsci-12-06831),[120](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B120-applsci-12-06831),[122](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B122-applsci-12-06831),[137](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B137-applsci-12-06831),[142](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B142-applsci-12-06831),[144](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B144-applsci-12-06831),[152](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B152-applsci-12-06831),[179](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B179-applsci-12-06831)] | Accuracy | $A c c u r a c y = \frac{X_{P r e d}}{X_{G T}}$ | $X_{P r e d}$ is the number of successes and $X_{G T}$ is the ground truth. |
| [[95](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B95-applsci-12-06831),[103](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B103-applsci-12-06831),[104](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B104-applsci-12-06831),[105](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B105-applsci-12-06831),[111](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B111-applsci-12-06831),[112](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B112-applsci-12-06831)] | mIoU | $I o U = \frac{R e g i o n o f i n t e r s e c t i o n}{R e g i o n o f u n i o n}$$m I o U = \frac{1}{k + 1} \sum_{n}^{k} \frac{T P}{T P + F P + F N}$ | - |
| [[34](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B34-applsci-12-06831),[63](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B63-applsci-12-06831),[77](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B77-applsci-12-06831)] | FNR | $F N R = \frac{F N}{T P + F N}$ | - |
| [[35](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B35-applsci-12-06831),[42](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B42-applsci-12-06831),[45](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B45-applsci-12-06831),[56](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B56-applsci-12-06831),[73](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B73-applsci-12-06831),[75](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B75-applsci-12-06831),[92](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B92-applsci-12-06831)] | mAP | $m A P = \frac{1}{n} \sum_{k = 1}^{k = n} \left(\left(R \left(k\right) - R \left(k + 1\right)\right) * P\right)$ | k denotes each episode and n is the total episodes. |
| [[65](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B65-applsci-12-06831),[66](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B66-applsci-12-06831),[68](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B68-applsci-12-06831),[76](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B76-applsci-12-06831),[78](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B78-applsci-12-06831)] | Log Average Miss Rate ($L o g_{A M R}$) | $L o g_{A M R} = e x p \left(\frac{1}{n} \sum_{i = 1}^{n} ln a_{i}\right)$ | $a_{i}$ is the series positive values correlated with the missing rate. |
| [[51](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B51-applsci-12-06831),[55](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B55-applsci-12-06831),[81](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B81-applsci-12-06831),[116](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B116-applsci-12-06831)] | Area Under Curve (AUC) | $A U C = \int T P R d \left(F P R\right)$ | TPR is the true positive rate, and FPR is the false positive rate. |
| [[83](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B83-applsci-12-06831),[89](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B89-applsci-12-06831),[131](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B131-applsci-12-06831)] | Lateral Error | Δy = $\Delta y_{r} + \left(L * \epsilon_{r}\right)$ | $\Delta y_{r}$ is center of gravity, $\epsilon_{r}$ is yaw angle towards the road and $L$ is the distance. |
| [[121](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B121-applsci-12-06831),[138](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B138-applsci-12-06831),[139](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B139-applsci-12-06831),[142](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B142-applsci-12-06831)] | Reward (r) | $r = \left{\begin{matrix} m i n \left(t_{1} , t_{2}\right) \\ i f l = 1 a n d d_{1} , d_{2} > 3 \\ m i n \left(t_{3} , t_{4}\right) \\ i f l = 2 a n d d_{1} , d_{2} > 3 \\ - 5 e l s e \end{matrix}\right$$w h e r e , t_{n} = \frac{d_{n} - d_{s n}}{v_{n}}$ | $t_{n}$ is dimension of state, $d_{n}$ is measured distance, $d_{s n}$ is safe distance, $l$ is overtaking lane and $v_{n}$ is the longitudinal velocity. Here, n = 1,2 refers host and lead vehicle in driving lane and n = 3,4 refers to overtaking lane. |
| [[123](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B123-applsci-12-06831),[126](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B126-applsci-12-06831),[128](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B128-applsci-12-06831),[130](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B130-applsci-12-06831),[136](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B136-applsci-12-06831)] | Collision Rate ($C_{r a t e}$) | $C_{r a t e} = \frac{N_{c o l}}{C_{l a p}}$ | $N_{c o l}$ is the total collision number while completing total $C_{l a p}$ laps. |
| [[102](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B102-applsci-12-06831),[124](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B124-applsci-12-06831),[127](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B127-applsci-12-06831),[134](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B134-applsci-12-06831),[165](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B165-applsci-12-06831)] | Success Rate (SR) | $S R = \frac{S u c c e s s C o u n t s}{T o t a l T e s t N u m b e r} * 100 \%$ | - |
| [[129](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B129-applsci-12-06831),[135](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B135-applsci-12-06831)] | Safety Index (SI) | $\bar{s} = \frac{N_{w c}}{N_{e p}}$ | $N_{w c}$ is total episode without collision and $N_{e p}$ is the total episodes. |
| [[133](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B133-applsci-12-06831),[147](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B147-applsci-12-06831),[148](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B148-applsci-12-06831),[151](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B151-applsci-12-06831),[158](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B158-applsci-12-06831)] | MAD/MAE | $M A E / M A D = \frac{1}{n} \sum_{i = 1}^{n} \left|x_{i} - \left(\hat{x}\right)_{i}\right|$ | $x_{i}$ and $\left(\hat{x}\right)_{i}$ are the real and predicted value, respectively, and $n$ is the total episodes. |
| [[137](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B137-applsci-12-06831)] | Specificity (SPC) | $S P C = \frac{T N}{F P + T N}$ | $T N$ is the true negative value. |
| [[150](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B150-applsci-12-06831),[159](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B159-applsci-12-06831)] | Autonomy value (Av) | $A v = \left(1 - \left(\frac{N_{i}}{T_{e}}\right) * 6\right) * 100 \%$ | $N_{i}$ is total interventions and $T_{e}$ is elapsed time. |
| [[153](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B153-applsci-12-06831),[154](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B154-applsci-12-06831),[155](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B155-applsci-12-06831),[156](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B156-applsci-12-06831),[166](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B166-applsci-12-06831),[167](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B167-applsci-12-06831),[168](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B168-applsci-12-06831)] | RMSE | $R M S E = \sqrt{\frac{\sum_{i = 1}^{n} \left(x_{i} - \hat{x_{i}}\right)^{2}}{n}}$ | $x_{i}$ is the ground truth and $\hat{x_{i}}$ is the predicted truth. |
| [[117](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12146831#B117-applsci-12-06831)] | p-Value | $Z = \frac{y^{'} - y^{0}}{\frac{\sqrt{y^{0} \left(1 - y^{0}\right)}}{c}}$ | $y^{'}$ is the sample proportion, $y^{0}$ is the assumed proportion, $n$ is sample size. |
